{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/3/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Check if last non-empty, non-comment line looks like a trailer.\n\t// Git trailers must be in a separate paragraph (after a blank line).\n\t// A single-paragraph message (e.g., just a subject line) cannot have trailers,\n\t// even if the subject contains \": \" (like conventional commits: \"docs: Add foo\").\n\thasTrailers := false\n\tseenBlankLine := false\n\tfor i := len(lines) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {\n\t\tline := strings.TrimSpace(lines[i])\n\t\tif line == \"\" {\n\t\t\tseenBlankLine = true\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Skip git comment lines\n\t\tif strings.HasPrefix(line, \"#\") {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif strings.Contains(line, \": \") {\n\t\t\thasTrailers = true\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t\t// Non-comment, non-trailer line found - no existing trailers\n\t\tbreak\n\t}\n\t// Trailers require a preceding blank line to be a separate paragraph\n\tif !seenBlankLine {\n\t\thasTrailers = false\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Check if the message already ends with a trailer paragraph.\n\t// Git trailers must be in a separate paragraph (preceded by a blank line).\n\t// A single-paragraph message (e.g., just a subject line) cannot have trailers,\n\t// even if the subject contains \": \" (like conventional commits: \"docs: Add foo\").\n\t//\n\t// Scan from the bottom: find the last paragraph of non-comment content,\n\t// then check if it looks like trailers AND has a blank line above it.\n\thasTrailers := false\n\ti := len(lines) - 1\n\n\t// Skip trailing comment lines\n\tfor i >= 0 && strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimSpace(lines[i]), \"#\") {\n\t\ti--\n\t}\n\n\t// Check if the last non-comment line looks like a trailer\n\tif i >= 0 {\n\t\tline := strings.TrimSpace(lines[i])\n\t\tif line != \"\" && strings.Contains(line, \": \") {\n\t\t\t// Found a trailer-like line. Now scan upward past the trailer block\n\t\t\t// to verify there's a blank line (paragraph separator) above it.\n\t\t\tfor i > 0 {\n\t\t\t\ti--\n\t\t\t\tabove := strings.TrimSpace(lines[i])\n\t\t\t\tif strings.HasPrefix(above, \"#\") {\n\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tif above == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\t\t// Blank line found above trailer block — real trailers\n\t\t\t\t\thasTrailers = true\n\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tif !strings.Contains(above, \": \") {\n\t\t\t\t\t// Non-trailer, non-blank line — this is message body, not trailers\n\t\t\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t// Another trailer-like line, keep scanning upward\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}"
}

> AGENT

Draft PR created: https://github.com/entireio/cli/pull/342

Two fixes in this PR:
1. **Transcript parsing**: Custom `UnmarshalJSON` on `GeminiMessage` handles both `"content": "string"` and `"content": [{"text": "..."}]` formats
2. **Trailer formatting**: `addCheckpointTrailer` no longer falsely detects conventional commit subjects (like `docs: Add foo`) as existing trailers — it now verifies a blank line exists above the trailer-like block before treating it as an existing trailer paragraph

+	// Check if there's new content to condense. Two cases:
+	// 1. Transcript has grown since last condensation (new prompts/responses)
+	// 2. FilesTouched has files not yet committed (carry-forward scenario)
+	//
+	// For PrepareCommitMsg context, we verify staged files overlap with session's files
+	// using content-aware matching to detect reverted files.
+	// For PostCommit context, getStagedFiles() is empty (files already committed),
+	// so we return true and let the caller do the overlap check via filesOverlapWithContent.
+
+	hasTranscriptGrowth := transcriptLines > state.CheckpointTranscriptStart
+	hasUncommittedFiles := len(state.FilesTouched) > 0
+
+	if !hasTranscriptGrowth && !hasUncommittedFiles {
+		return false, nil // No new content and no carry-forward files
+	}
+
+	// Check if staged files overlap with session's files with content-aware matching.
+	// This is primarily for PrepareCommitMsg; in PostCommit, stagedFiles is empty.
+	stagedFiles := getStagedFiles(repo)
+	if len(stagedFiles) > 0 {
+		return stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo, tree, stagedFiles, state.FilesTouched), nil
+	}
+
+	// No staged files - either PostCommit context or edge case.
+	// Return transcript growth status. For PostCommit with hasTranscriptFile=true,
+	// if there's no transcript growth, the session hasn't done new work since last checkpoint.
+	// (Carry-forward creates a shadow branch WITHOUT transcript, handled in the block above.)
+	return hasTranscriptGrowth, nil
 }
 
 // sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript checks if a session has new content

> AGENT

Here's the comparison between our PR #342 and Stefan's PR #343:

**Overlap:**
- Both touch `manual_commit_hooks.go` and `manual_commit_test.go`
- Both aim to fix Gemini checkpointing

**What #343 does that #342 doesn't:**
- Large E2E test suite for Gemini (new `e2e_test/` files, `scenario_subagent_test.go`)
- Content overlap improvements (`content_overlap.go` - smarter `hasSignificantContentOverlap`, partial staging detection)
- `CondenseSession` now takes `committedFiles` param to filter `files_touched` per commit (1:1 checkpoint model)
- `sessionHasNewContent` rewritten with more nuanced staged vs post-commit logic
- Carry-forward fixes, auto-commit fixes
- CLAUDE.md documentation updates

**What #342 does that #343 doesn't:**
- **Gemini transcript parsing fix** (`UnmarshalJSON` for array content) - #343 doesn't touch `geminicli/transcript.go` at all. This is the root cause fix.
- **Trailer blank-line fix** (`addCheckpointTrailer` false positive on conventional commit subjects) - #343 doesn't fix this either.

**Verdict:** The two PRs are **complementary, not conflicting**. #343 fixes E2E infrastructure, content overlap matching, and carry-forward logic. #342 fixes the two root-cause Gemini bugs (transcript parsing + trailer formatting) that #343 doesn't address. Both are needed. There will be minor merge conflicts in `manual_commit_hooks.go` and `manual_commit_test.go` but they should be straightforward to resolve since the changes are in different areas of those files.

+func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
+	// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p "<prompt>" --approval-mode auto_edit --allowed-tools <tools>
+	// --approval-mode auto_edit: auto-approves edit tools (write_file, replace) while prompting for others
+	// --allowed-tools: comma-separated list of tools that bypass confirmation (e.g., git commands)
+	args := []string{
+		"-m", r.Model,
+		"-p", prompt,
+		"--approval-mode", "auto_edit",
+	}
+
+	// Add default git tools that should be allowed without confirmation
+	defaultAllowedTools := []string{
+		"ShellTool(git status)",
+		"ShellTool(git add)",
+		"ShellTool(git commit)",
+		"ShellTool(git diff)",
+		"ShellTool(git log)",
+	}
+
+	// Merge with any additional tools passed in
+	allTools := append(defaultAllowedTools, tools...)
+	if len(allTools) > 0 {
+		args = append(args, "--allowed-tools", strings.Join(allTools, ","))
+	}
+
+	// Create context with timeout
+	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
+	defer cancel()
+
+	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
+	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", args...)
+	cmd.Dir = workDir
+
+	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
+	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
+	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
+
+	start := time.Now()
+	err := cmd.Run()
+	duration := time.Since(start)
+
+	result := &AgentResult{
+		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
+		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
+		Duration: duration,
+	}
+
+	if err != nil {
+		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
+		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
+			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
+		} else {
+			result.ExitCode = -1
+		}
+		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
+		return result, err
+	}
+
+	result.ExitCode = 0
+	return result, nil
 }

> AGENT

Here's the answer to your question about how Stefan's e2e tests pass without fixing the transcript parsing root cause:

## How Stefan's Gemini E2E Tests Pass

There are three key reasons:

### 1. Agent commits bypass content detection entirely

The `PrepareCommitMsg` hook in `manual_commit_hooks.go` has a **fast path** (around line 275):

```go
if !hasTTY() && phase.IsActive() {
    return addTrailerForAgentCommit(...)
}
```

When the Gemini agent creates a commit (no TTY, session is ACTIVE), it skips the entire `sessionHasNewContent()` check — which is the codepath that calls `countTranscriptItems()` → `ParseTranscript()` and fails. So the `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer gets added regardless of whether the transcript can be parsed.

### 2. Relaxed checkpoint validation for non-Claude agents

Stefan's `ValidateCheckpoint` in `testenv.go` only enforces JSONL format validation for Claude Code agents. For Gemini, it accepts any content in the transcript file. So even if the transcript data is stored incorrectly or partially, the validation passes.

### 3. `GetLatestCheckpointID` uses naive string scanning

Instead of using `git interpret-trailers` or proper git trailer parsing, the e2e test helper scans commit message lines looking for the `Entire-Checkpoint:` prefix with simple string matching. This works even if the trailer isn't properly formatted (e.g., missing blank line separator) — which is exactly the bug we fixed.

## Why our fixes are still needed

Stefan's e2e tests only exercise the **agent commit** path. Our fixes address the **user commit** path and broader pipeline:

1. **Transcript parsing fix** — needed for:
   - `countTranscriptItems()` during user commits (post-commit condensation)
   - `ExtractUserPrompts()` for session log display
   - `ExtractModifiedFiles()` for file tracking
   - Any future code that reads Gemini transcripts

2. **Trailer formatting fix** — needed for:
   - Git tooling that relies on proper trailer parsing (`git log --format='%(trailers)'`)
   - The `git interpret-trailers` command
   - Any tool that expects RFC 822-style trailers with blank line separation

In short: Stefan's tests pass because they test a codepath that sidesteps both bugs. The bugs still manifest on the user-commit codepath (when a human runs `git commit` with a Gemini session active).

+	// Check if there's new content to condense. Two cases:

+	// using content-aware matching to detect reverted files.
+	// For PostCommit context, getStagedFiles() is empty (files already committed),
+	// so we return true and let the caller do the overlap check via filesOverlapWithContent.
+
+	hasTranscriptGrowth := transcriptLines > state.CheckpointTranscriptStart
+	hasUncommittedFiles := len(state.FilesTouched) > 0
+
+	if !hasTranscriptGrowth && !hasUncommittedFiles {
+		return false, nil // No new content and no carry-forward files

+	// Check if staged files overlap with session's files with content-aware matching.

+	}
+

+	return hasTranscriptGrowth, nil

> AGENT

The bugbot is flagging that we removed the `StepCount > 0 || len(state.FilesTouched) > 0` guard for ACTIVE sessions in `PostCommit`. Stefan's PR does **not** address this — his changes are to `sessionHasNewContent`, `condenseAndUpdateState` signatures, and `CondenseSession`, but not the ACTIVE session path we changed.

The bugbot has a valid point for **concurrent sessions**: if sessions A and B are both ACTIVE, and only A has content, `PrepareCommitMsg` adds the trailer because of A. But our change makes B also condense (empty), which could create a spurious checkpoint for B.

The fix is simple — restore the guard while keeping our improved comment:

plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch)\n\t_, err = repo.Reference(refName, true)\n\tassert.NoError(t, err,\n\t\t\"shadow branch should be preserved when an uncondensed session remains\")\n}"

> AGENT

Done. The bugbot's concern was valid and **not** addressed by Stefan's PR. Here's what I did:

1. **Restored the guard** in `PostCommit`: ACTIVE sessions now only set `hasNew = true` when `StepCount > 0 || len(FilesTouched) > 0`, preventing empty concurrent sessions from being spuriously condensed.

2. **Updated the test** from `TestPostCommit_ActiveSessionAlwaysCondenses` (which tested the wrong behavior) to `TestPostCommit_EmptyActiveSessionSkipsCondensation` — verifying that an empty ACTIVE session is skipped while a concurrent session with content still condenses correctly.

3. **Pushed** (83c840a2) and **replied** to the bugbot thread.

	// LastCheckpointID is set after condensation completes.
	for _, state := range sessions {
		if state.BaseCommit != currentHead {
			continue

		var cpID id.CheckpointID

```
	var pendingMigrations []pendingMigration

	// Pass 1: Run transitions and dispatch condensation/discard actions.
	// Defer migration actions to pass 2.
	for _, state := range sessions {
		shadowBranchName := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)

		// Check for new content (needed for TransitionContext and condensation).
		// Fail-open: if content check errors, assume new content exists so we
		// don't silently skip data that should have been condensed.
		hasNew, contentErr := s.sessionHasNewContent(repo, state)
		if contentErr != nil {
			hasNew = true
			logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: error checking session content, assuming new content",
				slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
				slog.String("error", contentErr.Error()),
			)
		}
		transitionCtx.HasFilesTouched = len(state.FilesTouched) > 0

		// Run the state machine transition
		remaining := TransitionAndLog(state, session.EventGitCommit, transitionCtx)

		// Dispatch strategy-specific actions.
		// Each branch handles its own BaseCommit update so there is no
		// fallthrough conditional at the end. On condensation failure,
		// BaseCommit is intentionally NOT updated to preserve access to
		// the shadow branch (which is named after the old BaseCommit).
		for _, action := range remaining {
			switch action {
			case session.ActionCondense:
				if hasNew {
					s.condenseAndUpdateState(logCtx, repo, checkpointID, state, head, shadowBranchName, shadowBranchesToDelete)
					// condenseAndUpdateState updates BaseCommit on success.
					// On failure, BaseCommit is preserved so the shadow branch remains accessible.
				} else {
					// No new content to condense — just update BaseCommit
					s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)
				}
			case session.ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:
				// The state machine already gates this action on HasFilesTouched,
				// but hasNew is an additional content-level check (transcript has
				// new content beyond what was previously condensed).
				if len(state.FilesTouched) > 0 && hasNew {
					s.condenseAndUpdateState(logCtx, repo, checkpointID, state, head, shadowBranchName, shadowBranchesToDelete)
					// On failure, BaseCommit is preserved (same as ActionCondense).
				} else {
					s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)
				}
			case session.ActionDiscardIfNoFiles:
				if len(state.FilesTouched) == 0 {
					logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: skipping empty ended session (no files to condense)",
						slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
					)
				}
				s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)
```

> AGENT

Confirmed — reproduces locally now. The problem is clear: on main, ACTIVE sessions call `sessionHasNewContent()` for **all** sessions (no StepCount/FilesTouched guard). Our guard skips condensation when StepCount=0 and FilesTouched is empty, but these integration tests have ACTIVE sessions that commit mid-turn before any checkpoint is saved (StepCount=0) and before FilesTouched is populated.

The right fix: keep the original main behavior for ACTIVE sessions — always call `sessionHasNewContent()` — but now that we've fixed Gemini transcript parsing, it'll work correctly for both agents.

   893→// sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript checks if a session has new content
   894→// by examining the live transcript file. This is used when no shadow branch exists
   895→// (i.e., no Stop has happened yet) but the agent may have done work.
   896→//
   897→// Returns true if:
   898→//  1. The transcript has grown since the last condensation, AND
   899→//  2. The new transcript portion contains file modifications, AND
   900→//  3. At least one modified file overlaps with the currently staged files
   901→//
   902→// The overlap check ensures we don't add checkpoint trailers to commits that are
   903→// unrelated to the agent's recent changes.
   904→//
   905→// This handles the scenario where the agent commits mid-session before Stop.
   906→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
   907→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   908→
   909→	modifiedFiles, ok := s.extractNewModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript(state)
   910→	if !ok || len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {
   911→		return false, nil
   912→	}
   913→
   914→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: found file modifications",
   915→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   916→		slog.Int("modified_files", len(modifiedFiles)),
   917→	)
   918→
   919→	// Check if any modified files overlap with currently staged files
   920→	// This ensures we only add checkpoint trailers to commits that include
   921→	// files the agent actually modified
   922→	stagedFiles := getStagedFiles(repo)
   923→
   924→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: comparing staged vs modified",
   925→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   926→		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
   927→		slog.Int("modified_files", len(modifiedFiles)),
   928→	)
   929→
   930→	if !hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, modifiedFiles) {
   931→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: no overlap between staged and modified files",
   932→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   933→		)
   934→		return false, nil // No overlap - staged files are unrelated to agent's work
   935→	}
   936→
   937→	return true, nil
   938→}
   939→
   940→// extractFilesFromLiveTranscript extracts modified file paths from the live transcript.
   941→// Returns empty slice if extraction fails (fail-open behavior for hooks).
   942→// Extracts ALL files from the transcript (offset 0) because this is used for carry-forward
   943→// computation which needs to know all files touched, not just new ones.
   944→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) extractFilesFromLiveTranscript(state *SessionState) []string {
   945→	return s.extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript(state, 0)
   946→}
   947→

   949→// transcript that are NEW since the last condensation. Returns the normalized file list

Author: Alex Ong <alex@entire.io>
Date:   Sun Feb 15 15:55:29 2026 +1100

    Trust PrepareCommitMsg trailer for ACTIVE session condensation
    
    For ACTIVE sessions in PostCommit, always set hasNew=true instead of
    re-validating via transcript analysis. PrepareCommitMsg already validates
    the trailer (unconditionally for no-TTY/agent commits, via content
    detection for TTY/human commits). Transcript-based re-validation is
    unreliable because subagent transcripts may not be available at
    PostCommit time (subagent still running).
    
    Removes sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles which is now unused.

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. The original code had three paths:

1. **ACTIVE + has content** (`StepCount > 0 || FilesTouched > 0`): `hasNew = true`
2. **ACTIVE + no content**: fallback to `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` (checks live transcript against **committed** files, not staged)
3. **Non-ACTIVE**: `sessionHasNewContent` (checks against staged files)

Our commit `cdc5c7ee` deleted path #2 and simplified to just `hasNew = true`. Now we need to restore path #2. Let me check if `committedFileSet` is still available.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/3/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "// extractFilesFromLiveTranscript extracts modified file paths from the live transcript.",
  "new_string": "// sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles checks if a session's live transcript mentions\n// files that overlap with the just-committed files. This is used in PostCommit for\n// ACTIVE sessions with no checkpoints yet (StepCount=0, no FilesTouched), where\n// getStagedFiles returns empty because files are already committed.\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles(state *SessionState, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) bool {\n\tlogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), \"checkpoint\")\n\n\tmodifiedFiles, ok := s.extractNewModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript(state)\n\tif !ok || len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"committed files check: found file modifications\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"modified_files\", len(modifiedFiles)),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"committed_files\", len(committedFiles)),\n\t)\n\n\t// Check if any modified files overlap with committed files\n\tfor _, f := range modifiedFiles {\n\t\tif _, ok := committedFiles[f]; ok {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"committed files check: no overlap between committed and modified files\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t)\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// extractFilesFromLiveTranscript extracts modified file paths from the live transcript."
}

That's a lot of history. The three commits that are **ours** (this PR's work):

1. **`e8ec608b`** — fix: handle array content format in Gemini transcript parsing
2. **`dab5bac2`** — fix: trailer not separated by blank line for single-line commits
3. **`aeb8242c`** — fix: restore StepCount/FilesTouched guard for ACTIVE sessions in PostCommit

So the `comm` missed those — both #342 and #343 touch the same three core strategy files. Here's the picture:

| PR | What it does | Key files |
|----|-------------|-----------|
| **#323** | Subagent file modifications fix (ENT-297) | hooks.go, condensation.go, integration tests |
| **#342** (ours) | Gemini transcript parsing + trailer formatting | **Built on #323** — includes all its commits. Adds: transcript.go fix, hooks.go trailer fix, hooks.go PostCommit guard |
| **#343** (soph) | E2E tests + content-overlap improvements | hooks.go (`sessionHasNewContent`, `condenseAndUpdateState` signature), condensation.go, content_overlap.go (new), e2e tests |

Key relationships:

- **#342 is a superset of #323** — our branch was forked from #323's branch. If #323 merges first, #342 can rebase down to just our 3 commits.
- **#342 and #343 conflict on `hooks.go`** — both change `sessionHasNewContent` and PostCommit logic. Soph makes `sessionHasNewContent` PostCommit-aware (returns `hasTranscriptGrowth` when no staged files). We re-added `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` as a separate function. These solve the same problem differently.
- **#323 and #343 are mostly independent** — minimal overlap (just `auto_commit.go`).

The natural sequence would be:

1. **#323 first** — foundational subagent fix, already reviewed
2. **#343 second** — soph's E2E + content-overlap (independent from #323)
3. **Rebase #342 onto main** — strip out #323's commits, resolve conflicts with #343's `sessionHasNewContent` changes, and drop our `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` since soph's approach handles it. Our PR shrinks to just the Gemini transcript parsing fix + trailer fix.

> AGENT

Yeah, bugbot is flagging that `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` returns `false` when transcript extraction fails. But this is the fail-**closed** path for a narrow edge case — an ACTIVE session with zero checkpoints and zero tracked files where the live transcript is also unreadable. In that scenario there's genuinely nothing to condense; the trailer was added by `PrepareCommitMsg` for a *different* session's content.

The alternative (fail-open / return `true`) would cause empty sessions to attempt condensation with no data, which is worse.

I'd reply acknowledging it and dismissing. Want me to?

> AGENT

Failure conditions for `extractNewModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript` returning `(nil, false)`:

1. **`state.TranscriptPath == ""`** — session hasn't recorded a transcript path yet
2. **`state.AgentType == ""`** — session hasn't recorded its agent type yet
3. **`agent.GetByAgentType()` fails** — unknown agent type (shouldn't happen in practice)
4. **Agent doesn't implement `TranscriptAnalyzer`** — agent can't parse transcripts
5. **`GetTranscriptPosition()` fails** — transcript file doesn't exist or can't be read (e.g., agent hasn't written anything yet)

Cases 1, 2, and 5 are the realistic ones — they all mean the session is so early that no transcript exists yet. Which is exactly the "empty ACTIVE session" scenario where there's nothing to condense anyway.

So bugbot's concern is theoretical — the trailer points to another session's checkpoint, not this empty one's. The empty session correctly skips condensation because it has no data to write.

> AGENT

The logs tell the story clearly. Let me trace the timeline:

1. **user-prompt-submit** → session ACTIVE, `StepCount=0`, `FilesTouched=[]`
2. **pre-task** → subagent starts
3. **Subagent commits** `docs/red.md` → triggers prepare-commit-msg → trailer added
4. **post-commit** → session is ACTIVE, `StepCount=0`, `FilesTouched=[]`
   - Falls into our `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` path
   - Reads **main agent's** transcript — finds no file modification tool calls (the *subagent* did the work, not the main agent)
   - Returns `false` → **no condensation** → just updates BaseCommit
5. **post-task** → subagent finishes
6. **stop** → checkpoint saved to shadow branch, but BaseCommit already moved past the commit

Line 19 confirms it: `files_touched_before=0, files=null` — no condensation.

This is the exact bug that `cdc5c7ee` ("Trust PrepareCommitMsg trailer") originally fixed. We reintroduced it by restoring the `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` fallback. The fundamental issue: for ACTIVE sessions, transcript-based re-validation can't see subagent file modifications because they're in a separate transcript.

The fix: go back to `hasNew = true` for ACTIVE sessions. The bugbot's concurrent-session concern is a cosmetic issue (empty checkpoint); this is a real data-loss regression.

   973→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles(state *SessionState, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) bool {
   974→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   975→

   996→	)
   997→	return false
   998→}

> AGENT

Yes, that's the tradeoff. If sessions A (has work) and B (empty, just started) are both ACTIVE on the same commit, B will also condense — producing a checkpoint entry with just its initial prompt and no real content, linked to a commit it didn't contribute to.

The question is which is worse:
1. **Empty checkpoint for concurrent session** (cosmetic — extra metadata entry with no real content)
2. **Subagent commit skips condensation entirely** (data loss — session work never gets linked to the commit)

The root cause is that we can't distinguish "this ACTIVE session's subagent made this commit" from "another ACTIVE session's subagent made this commit" at PostCommit time. We only know the commit has a trailer.

One possible middle ground: instead of `hasNew = true`, we could check if the session has a **transcript path** set (meaning the agent is actually running), and only then trust the trailer. An empty concurrent session that just started might not have a transcript yet. But that's fragile — the transcript path is set at session-start.

What's your take — is the concurrent session case something that actually happens in practice, or is it mostly theoretical?

id: toolu_01RHCaoJnD6UM48edPznXVWu
```
     1→# Known Limitations
     2→
     3→This document describes known limitations of the Entire CLI.
     4→
     5→## Git Operations
     6→
     7→### Amending Commits with `-m` Flag
     8→
     9→When you amend a commit using `git commit --amend -m "new message"`, the `-m` flag replaces the entire message including any `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer. Git passes `source="message"` (not `"commit"`) to the prepare-commit-msg hook, so the amend-specific trailer preservation logic is bypassed.
    10→
    11→**However, the trailer is automatically restored** if `LastCheckpointID` exists in session state (set during the original condensation). This means `git commit --amend -m "..."` preserves the checkpoint link in most cases, including when Claude does the amend in a non-interactive environment.
    12→
    13→The only case where the link is lost is when `-m` is used with genuinely *new* content (no prior condensation) and `/dev/tty` is not available for the interactive confirmation prompt.
    14→
    15→**Tracked in:** [ENT-161](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-161)
    16→
    17→### Git GC Can Corrupt Worktree Indexes
    18→
    19→When using git worktrees, `git gc --auto` can corrupt a worktree's index by pruning loose objects that the worktree's index cache-tree references. This manifests as:
    20→
    21→```
    22→fatal: unable to read <hash>
    23→error: invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree of .git/worktrees/<n>/index
    24→```
    25→
    26→**Root cause:** Checkpoint saves use go-git's `SetEncodedObject` which creates loose objects. When the count exceeds the `gc.auto` threshold (default 6700), any git operation (e.g., VS Code or Sourcetree background fetch) triggers `git gc --auto`. GC doesn't fully account for worktree index references when pruning, so objects get deleted while the worktree index still points to them.
    27→
    28→**Impact:**
    29→- `git status` fails in the affected worktree
    30→- Staged changes in the worktree are lost
    31→
    32→**Recovery:**
    33→```bash
    34→# In the affected worktree:
    35→git read-tree HEAD
    36→```
    37→This rebuilds the index from HEAD. Any previously staged changes will need to be re-staged.
    38→
    39→**Prevention:** Disable auto-GC and run it manually after commits (when indexes are clean):
    40→```bash
    41→git config gc.auto 0
    42→```
    43→
    44→**Tracked in:** [ENT-241](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-241)
    45→
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "**Tracked in:** [ENT-241](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-241)\n\n### Concurrent ACTIVE Sessions May Produce Spurious Checkpoints\n\nWhen multiple sessions are ACTIVE in the same directory and one session's agent (or subagent) makes a commit, **all** ACTIVE sessions are condensed — including sessions that didn't contribute to the commit. This can produce checkpoint entries with minimal content (e.g., just the initial prompt) linked to a commit the session didn't work on.\n\n**Why:** PostCommit sets `hasNew=true` for all ACTIVE sessions. We cannot re-validate via transcript analysis because subagent file modifications live in a separate transcript from the main agent's. Attempting to re-validate causes subagent commits to skip condensation entirely (data loss), which is worse than a spurious empty checkpoint.\n\n**Impact:** Cosmetic — extra metadata entries on `entire/checkpoints/v1` with minimal content. No data loss or corruption.\n\n**Workaround:** Use separate git worktrees for concurrent sessions. Each worktree gets its own shadow branch namespace, so sessions in different worktrees don't interfere."
}
